The 2012 balance of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia, which we are considering, did not want this coalition, nor was it a balance that reduces the expenditure side of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia for 2012, but is necessary and is the only way. We did not want a law to balance public finances, but it is a necessary and only way we see it. We wanted to start implementing our programme, the coalition contract, the Slovenia Treaty 2012-2015 as soon as possible in the early 1990s, since the five-party coalition took responsibility for the leadership of the country. The main reason for the necessity of adopting the 2012 budget rebalancing of the Republic of Slovenia is the unrealised planned expenditure and the pre-optimistic estimated revenue of the adopted budget of the Republic of Slovenia for this year, which was adopted in November 2010. In the 2012 adopted budget, the revenue of the State budget for 2012 was estimated at EUR 8 000 695 million, and the 2012 revised revenue estimate based on Umar's spring forecast of the economic trends in March 2012 and the proposed tax legislation was only EUR 7 000 942 million, which is approximately at the level of realised revenue in 2007. In the 2012 budget adopted, total budget expenditure was planned at a total of EUR 10 000 118 million. The budget deficit for 2012 was estimated at EUR 1 000 423 million, or 3.6% of the gross domestic product forecast for 2012. At the current estimated 2012 budget revenue, the planned expenditure in the 2012 budget would amount to more than EUR 2 000 200 million, or more than 6 % of gross domestic product. The goal of the Government for 2012 is to achieve a reduction in the deficit of the State budget in 2012 to 3% of the gross domestic product by means of the rebalancing of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia. In order to achieve this objective, the 2012 state budget balance should reduce the total budget expenditure to around EUR 9 000 14 million, or EUR 104 million less than the budget. The increased indebtedness of the Republic of Slovenia over the past years and the worsening conditions of indebtedness in international and domestic financial markets have also been added to the weaker forecasts in GDP growth estimates. As a result of past, excessive country deficits exceeding 3% of gross domestic product, an excessive budgetary deficit procedure has been introduced for Slovenia by the European Commission. In accordance with the excessive budgetary deficit procedure, Slovenia must reduce the deficit of the wider sector by the following year, i.e. by 2013 below 3 % of GDP. An additional challenge in the preparation of the budget rebalancing is and will be the reorganisation of the Ministry and Government services in accordance with the Government of the Republic of Slovenia Act and the State Administration Act. Today, the Republic of Slovenia has the smallest government since we live in a single country. The effects will not only be direct positive financial in nature, but will also be positive in terms of reducing, shortening and simplifying bureaucratic procedures that have brought almost total blockade in some segments. In drawing up the proposal, the 2012 budget rebalancing expenditure was followed by the government's main objective for this year in order to achieve a reduction in the deficit of the State budget in 2012 to 3% of gross domestic product. In order to achieve this objective, the 2012 state budget rebalancing should reduce the total budget expenditure to around €9014 million, a decrease of €349 million in 2011 and a reduction of €104 million in 2012, according to the 2012 budget adopted. From today's perspective, it seems completely incomprehensible that the budget of the Republic of Slovenia with €1.4 billion has been adopted here in this house for 2012. At this point, ladies and gentlemen, I think it is worth recalling the debate on the Committee on Agriculture, where one of my fellow Members correctly observed that Slovenian farmers had stayed through the centuries, through all the different socio-political systems, irrespective of the state budgets that were sometimes more, sometimes less favourable to them. But I added and repeat, indeed, the Slovenian farmer stood still also and especially because he never excelled greed because he knew that he could only spend as much as he did, or in other words, because the Slovenian farmer always practiced a golden fiscal rule that limited his spending according to the summer. It may not have been written on the front of the farm, but it is a good, practical and only reasonable example, which should also be taken into account by Slovenian policy. Slovenia has been moving away from the strategic objectives of economic development and the well-being of the population in recent years. The decline in economic activity in Slovenia since the beginning of the economic crisis has been one of the largest in the European Union, which has pushed us from 91% to 85% of the average economic development of the members of the European Union, measured in gross domestic product per capita by purchasing power in 2010. However, further increases in the development gap continued in 2011. Despite measures aimed at mitigating the impact of the economic crisis on the social situation of the population, the economic situation has led to a reduction in the available income and thus a deterioration in the material well-being of the population. The economic and social situation requires a sustainable consolidation of public finances and a solid foundation for reviving economic activity, which will be more resistant to shocks and will enable jobs to be created. In New Slovenia, we claim that without consolidation, without balancing public finances, there will simply be no economic growth, so it is also quite understandable that the Slovenian economy supports the government and coalition in its efforts to balance public finances. Without structural adjustments, the development backlog will deepen and the tightening of the labour market will continue for longer, reducing the quality of life. The measures should therefore focus primarily on, firstly, the consolidation of public finances, secondly, the regulation of the financial sector and thirdly, the adjustment of the social protection system, the pension and health system, the system of long-term care and ways of delivering public services, which will, in financial and demographically modified circumstances, maintain the accessibility of public services, material standards and the quality of life at least at the current level. After economic development since 2008, Slovenia has been moving away from the average of the European Union countries, which it was already lagging behind in 2010 more than at the start of the implementation of the 2005 Slovenian Development Strategy. In recent years in Slovenia there has been a shift from achieving the central economic objective of the strategy for the development of Slovenia – to becoming an average European Union country by 2013, which will not only be temporary in nature. The reasons why, after a severe fall in gross domestic product at the beginning of the crisis, there was no more serious economic recovery, mainly from the domestic environment. The current economic and social situation dictates the earliest sustainable consolidation of public finances, the recovery of economic activity and the improvement of the labour market situation. Your Honor! Slovenia is an important member of the European Union. We are not a closed state or a closed economy, we are a country that wants to build a stable socio-market economy. Therefore, it is important that we send signals today, or at this time, abroad. For the past two years, we have been telling the truth and making false promises to balance public finances. And we didn't, even though we tried. We have received the lesson and I am sure that there is today a willingness in Slovenia to limit public spending. I am afraid that the referendums and sentences, before anything happens, do not affect the rise in Slovenia's credit ratings. The former Member and Minister Lahovnik cautions that reckless political moves and events such as the sentence will increase the cost of indebtedness, which will needlessly be reflected in the even higher interest costs that will be paid for public debt in the future. If this government package of measures in the referendum is rejected, we will send a clear message abroad that Slovenia is not prepared to limit public spending. Slovenia is still in a position to take action in time and avoid the Greek scenario. The blackest scenario that can happen in the coming months is that when the state tries again to issue bonds on international markets to cover part of the past debt, no one will be willing to buy these bonds due to high risk. At that moment, we will find ourselves in the scenario where Greece is today. The New Slovenia Group will support the 2012 budget rebalancing of the Republic of Slovenia, as well as the previous amendments signed by us. Saving measures, which are inevitable for balancing public finances, are not simple, but we hope to look everyone in the eye and explain their urgency. The standard we've been used to right now, and hopefully only temporarily, is no longer possible. We can't spend any more than we produce. With sobriety and patience, that must be understandable. It is for the patience of citizens and citizens from this city that I am asking. It's not easy, and we have everyone in mind when we decide on austerity measures. All this, in order to ensure a better and stable future for all, especially the generations that are coming. Thank you for your attention.

And this is how the state should behave. We all want more, we all want more for kindergartens, more for schools, more for development, more for economic incentives, we all want it, even in coalitions. But the fact is, we can just give as much as we can collect. In some ways, however, the state can raise money into its own budget. One option is, which the opposition is very much talking about, raising taxes, for example raising VAT. This is relatively simple, even fast. Another option is to increase our revenue in the budget to create new jobs. Of course, these new jobs cannot be created with words. We are no longer living in a time of socialism, when some party secretary said that from tomorrow he would be, for example, at the Titan Stone Factory, 100 more jobs. And they were. It did not matter whether these jobs were needed or not. That's the way it was done, but in these days we don't live anymore. The logic is that the more jobs there are, the more he will pay in the budget. But today, things are just the other way around. Instead of putting people into the budget when they are employed, the state is still giving them social support. If I touch, for example, the first option the opposition is talking about, it is an increase in taxes and an increase in VAT. In recent years, we have spent 2 billion euros more. This year we will spend even one billion euros more and more than we will collect in the budget. Calculations exist that if we raise value added tax for one percentage point, this would mean €120 million more. And if you follow me, one billion 120 million, that would mean that this hole would be filled with a VAT hike, so that this tax should be raised by 8%. Can you imagine? From current 20 to 28 percentage points. I don't know who could do it. And another thing to be pointed out about this is the so-called Laffer curve. The economists will know that very well. For my fellow Members, I have drawn up a graph where it is very good to see that raising taxes does not necessarily mean more revenue in the budget. When we reach a certain threshold, even if we are increasing taxes, the revenue in the budget is falling. You know, higher than taxes, the more people avoid paying taxes. If in this case it is not just an economic theory, I can substantiate this implementation with today's financial article, which has the title, maybe it flies to the opposition, especially to them, do not break it by raising the VAT. We have a concrete case in Spain when they raised VAT and the revenue in the budget went down. By raising VAT in some way we can alleviate the symptoms of the disease in the short term. Unfortunately, we do not eliminate the cause of the disease. After all, this means that our economy will still be sick if we just burden and burden it with new taxes. What did this coalition decide? This is a conceptual difference. The opposition is more inclined to raise taxes, we are trying to create better economic conditions. And what does it take to do? This is widely known, I am only stressing once again, for the first time, the tax burden and administrative burden on the entire Slovenian economic system should be lifted. Above all, it is necessary to have clear rules of the game. What does that mean? It's a state of law. Not that today some people don't pay bills and nothing just happens. And another key thing that this coalition is dealing with is access to funding resources. In what way Slovenian companies can get money to finance projects. Since the logic is this: if there is no money, there are no projects, if there are no projects, there are no new jobs, if there are no new jobs, he has not paid into the budget. All economists make it clear that the first step to freeing up the credit crunch, as is said to be modern, or to make it easier to access funding sources is to regulate public finances. And that's what we do with this balance. If we have unregulated public finances, we have poor credit ratings. Poor ratings mean higher and more expensive money we rent on foreign markets. After all, this is also more expensive money for commercial banks. And when we get to individual companies, that means higher interest rates and more difficult conditions for issuing loans. These are facts anyone can see if they want to see. I understand, however, that in these moments the coalition has placed a heavy burden on us, when what we have to do must be done and that the opposition is in a much easier position, when it can always say, but we would do even better, and we could do it differently. We will take this responsibility, support this rebalancing and I am sure that the situation in Slovenia will also start to improve at some point in time.

These are measures that increase the competitiveness of the economy and increase economic growth while at the same time recapitalising banks, I repeat, from private capital and not from tax money. All these measures do not address the deficit, but reduce it to the limit to which we can still be indebted. Let me give you some facts as to why I believe that these public finances need to be regulated. It is already worrying that Slovenia in Cyprus is the only country in the euro area that has not reduced the deficit in the last three years. It is also important that Slovenia committed itself already in 2009 to reducing the country's deficit below 3% of GDP by 2013 and, of course, did not do so, so it was definitely delayed in 2009, 2010 and 2011 from this point of view. We do not actually have the possibility of a gradual reduction now, but we need to reduce the deficit fully and within this promised deadline. I think it is of this, and here I agree with some representatives of the Government, also depends on the credibility of Slovenia. Why should austerity measures be taken now? I think that, otherwise, more serious measures will have to be taken in time if we are to even want to be a master in our own country. However, unlike the words of some who still have their heads in socialism and who are used to the old-fashioned printing of money, we do not have time. Why? Because the deadlines are linked to signed agreements at EU level. I don't think anyone wants us to have severe sanctions and more expensive loans in a few months because of non-compliance with the signed agreements or even if no one would ever approve of our loans again, which is obviously the story we're going to have to talk about. In this case, the State could even become insolvent, which at some point could mean that wages, such as public servants or pensions, would not be paid at all. We must also be aware that, despite the adoption of the balance that we are considering today, Slovenia will have to continue to be indebted. I believe that, therefore, we cannot or must not solve the crisis by further deliberation. We are currently in charge of something we are currently using, but we have not even created it. In the last three years, as has been said many times today, the public sector in the country has spent 20% more than it has created. If you spend 20% more each year than you create, how will you invest? It is likely that we will begin to save by ourselves to do everything, to come to the so-called green branch, to buy only urgently at that time, without luxury or missing or unnecessary purchases, only then will we start investing again. In short, we pay the price first, easier, cheaper and less painful. Public sector debt has increased over the last three years, from €8 billion to €17 billion, which I think some people are forgetting today. This means that public sector or state debt has doubled in a broader sense over the past three years, and we will pay so much more for interest. This year almost 700 million euros. Instead of giving these 700 million euros for development, education and other necessary things, including investments to increase the welfare of the country, we are paying this for interest. Thus, we will pay only the interest so much that we can pay all pensioners two additional pensions or all the students and all the students for this money for 2 years. If we are really disciplined, by 2015 we will approximate the budget and thus save the social state. If we do not, then Slovenia is before we are probably all afraid and no one wants to admit that we can start saying good-bye to the social state. ParlaMint-SI_2012-05-10-SDZ6-Izredna-13.ana.u120	Respected and respected! The Public Finance Balance Act is, as you yourself have already established, a law that is prepared in a way that, in relation to the budget and public finances, we probably have not yet prepared laws in Slovenia. This is a law that changes the individual provisions of 45 other laws. Why have we decided to take such an approach? This is because, as is generally known, most of the expenditure in Slovenian public spending is justified in one way or another by certain legal provisions. Various evaluations in the past have shown that between 80 and 90 percent of all expenditure is in the past, as has been said by laws. This has also often been used as an excuse to say that, for example, the Government has not prepared more radical measures, changes on the expenditure side and so on, because it has been said, the Government has hardly adapted itself because expenditure is based on legislation. Of course, we have said, but we are now in a situation where it is imperative to amend or adjust expenditure, and if it is necessary to amend legislation, then we will or will propose it to the National Assembly. We have done, as I have already said, several rounds of coordination with the ministries, ministers and ministers themselves have proposed certain adjustments, and there is no need for many to have a legal basis, because there is savings in individual programmes, projects, and many of them need such a legal basis. This law is now before you, even on the basis of negotiations with the social partners in recent days, even on the basis of hearing the arguments of the public, the opposition, the various stakeholders, also by the government coalition, has been amended. We believe that this is now a law that we can say that, in view of the situation in which we are, it is necessary and that, according to its actions, it is, however, at the same time, at the goal that we had to set ourselves, in the best possible way, balanced and sustainable. This law, the fact that such an intervention is such a change that it is necessary, without it, to enter into a situation which would be public finances, not only publicly and socially, but also economically difficult for Slovenia, this fact has been acknowledged in its own way by those who may be the most vulnerable or have or have others as representatives of social interests, is to say trade unions. Of course, they did not say that these measures were beautiful and that they were enthusiastic, but most of them today have committed themselves not to apply referendum requirements or support them if they do. Such a decision by trade unions shows that it is possible to convince the majority of the public with arguments that this fiscal adjustment is necessary, that it is substantially different from what we propose, that it would not be possible to prepare and implement at this time, and we expect this law to receive adequate support in the National Assembly as well. The financial effects of the law are estimated at around 500 million reductions in expenditure next year to around 750, because some measures are of a nature such that their impact will not be fully demonstrated until next year. What's been changed in the last few days with the amendments. There'll be another one today, too. In the light of the latest arrangements, we will ensure that we reduce some other items in the budget or find them in accordance with the capacity afforded by the Law on the implementation of the budget, the possibility of adjustments, redeployments, and in particular some reallocations from other items to payment items and other benefits from public sector work. All together, I have said this several times, the assessment is between one hundred and one hundred and fifty, maybe one hundred and thirty million, but this is something that can be ensured in the context of the envisaged deficit, and it is something that makes sense to adapt the proposal to the law, so that we have at least this assurance that the law will not be challenged to the referendum and that we can continue to do other things that the Government must do here. ParlaMint-SI_2012-05-10-SDZ6-Izredna-13.ana.u124	Hello again. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen of the Government, the distinguished Vice-President of the National Assembly! Dear fellow Members and Members.Oh, my God! The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy is at its 18th emergency meeting. 5. 2012 and the 18th emergency meeting. 5. On the basis of the third paragraph, in conjunction with the first and second paragraphs of Article 117 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, under the same item of the agenda, he considered three draft laws; namely the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Police, the summary procedure proposed by the Group of Members and Members of the First-signed Peter Wilfan, the Proposal for a Law on the Balance of Public Finance, the urgent procedure put forward by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia for consideration to the National Assembly and the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Income, the first hearing proposed to the Assembly by the Group of Members and Members of the First-signed Janko Vebro. In accordance with Rule 117 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee first decided to deal with those draft laws under the same agenda item under the provisions of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly on the urgent procedure for the adoption of legislation. Following the adoption of that decision, the representatives of the proponents, the Government and the Legal and Legislative Service presented the purpose and content of the solutions to the proposed laws or opinions and their compliance with the Constitution and the legal system. On this basis, the Committee decided to draw up the amended proposal for a law on the basis of the proposal for a law on public finances balance, an urgent procedure submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for consideration. On the basis of the discussion, the Committee also decided not to include any of the articles of the proposed Law on Police Amendments and Supplements in this draft law, which would supplement the Law on Police, and to include Article 1 of the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Income, Article 1 as a new 136.a. In the text of the Proposal for a Law on the Balance of Public Finance, supplemented by Article 1 of the Proposal for a Law on the Amendments and Supplements to the Income Act, the Committee then considered the proposal for a law under urgent procedure. The Legislative and Legal Service made a number of comments at the meeting, mainly in terms of the nomotechnics and the internal structure of the bill. It also clarified the principle of comment in its opinion, which draws attention to the importance and scope of the constitutionally guaranteed right after union freedom and the resulting autonomy of collective bargaining. With regard to the amendments of the coalition parliamentary groups, however, she felt that they were going in the direction of the recommendations made in her written opinion. In the debate on the articles of the bill and on the amendments tabled, the members and members of the Committee expressed different opinions, mainly based on the views of their respective groups. They were of the same opinion that the state of public finances should be gradually rectified and their balance achieved by 2015, thus restoring to the Republic of Slovenia its credibility, credibility and credibility both at home and in the European Union, while differences became clear on the question of how to achieve the structural balance of public finances. In the opinion of the members and members of the committee of opposition parliamentary groups, contrary to the Government, we should seek a solution in order to combine measures on the expenditure and revenue side. They advocated the preservation of the social state, social justice and the reduction of social differences as a basis for solidarity under the conditions of fiscal consolidation. The view was expressed that long-term solutions to the economic and financial crisis should be drawn up based on investment, investment in science and development and envisaged pension reforms, health and education reforms while preserving the social state. At the same time, it was pointed out that they were bringing proposed solutions to the amendment of individual laws in fact already under way to reform those areas. The debate focused on how financial consolidation could be achieved, without prejudice to specific areas. Specific and comprehensive responses were made by representatives of the Ministry of Finance and other relevant ministries to the questions raised in the debate on individual proposed legislative amendments or their provisions under the draft law. The Government is aware that the proposed measures in the Public Finance Balance Act can be put into question in terms of constitutionality, in particular in terms of prejudice to citizens' rights already acquired. In doing so, they stressed that the country is in a difficult financial and economic situation, and therefore fiscal balance is essential because without it there would be very severe consequences for the country. In the view of the Government, however, fiscal balance cannot be achieved without prejudice to certain rights already acquired, and the Constitutional Court will also have to judge in the case of requests for a constitutional review of the law when it is adopted. The Committee adopted coalition amendments prepared on the basis of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service and the coordination with the trade union partners, as well as some other amendments of the opposition parliamentary groups. The Committee voted on all articles of the proposal and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation is prepared and the amendments adopted are included. On the basis of the decisions taken, the committee concluded the legislative procedure for the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Police Act and the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Income.

However, for about the same amount, according to the previous Government, a child allowance should be increased for parents. If I say so, 210 euros will come from boarding school and some interstate bus plus urban transport will charge around 170 euros. Let me tell you one of my own experiences, I spoke to a mother yesterday who has a larger number of children — 7. As a result, her family budget has fallen by €350. The youngest child goes to high school and really says she doesn't know where to put him, because she can't afford a boarding school to go to that school she wants to go to. This law was the one that actually meant a radical incision into the material rights of the family. The burden of the crisis has also shifted the current opposition to the shoulders of the weak ones. The new Slovenia will therefore endeavour to make the law cited, although it has barely entered into force and the beneficiaries have not yet received all the decisions, so many complications have been corrected as soon as possible by providing at least minimum social benefits for poorer families and large families who will or will remain poor families because of these measures.

ParlaMint-SI_2012-05-10-SDZ6-Izredna-13.ana.u232	Nicely greeted! Thank you for that interesting question. I will be very happy to explain how we have thought about this article. This is what I can finally start with the EU Treaty, the Treaty on the Management of Fiscal Policy, a popular fiscal pact that has already been ratified by the National Assembly. Among other things, it says something, I will not literally quote, just after the memory, in that countries are also obliged to think about automatic repair mechanisms in case they exceed the deficit allowed. It is said that an automatic mechanism is something that triggers itself without having to take any additional, such as a legal act or anything like that. Let me remind you that we have been introducing something similar to this for a long time in our laws on the implementation of the budget. I think for at least eight, nine years, it's a constant practice of these laws. However, in terms of economic theory, these provisions are called automatic stabilisers — automatic self-repair mechanisms may be slightly better. It is, for example, provisions in terms of if this is necessary for the implementation of the budget, for the achievement of the target deficit and so on, for example, for the government itself, for example, to reduce the linear amount of spending rights on all items and so on. It is said that this logic has already been built in here. It is said that this thinking is not foreign to us, nor in this country, that we can incorporate into the law mechanisms which, under certain conditions, trigger themselves and interfere with the area of public finances. We also have other cases where the government has the legal authority to change certain taxes, of course to some extent prescribed, by its own decision. Mrs Bratuškova herself mentioned the case of excise duties, where this range, in which the government can change it itself, is limited. At the same time, when it is limited, it is the only limit that the government has. The Government may, on a proposal from the Minister of Finance, for example, determine excise duties on petrol entirely by its own discretion and is not bound by any instructions from the National Assembly, nor by any legal conditions. We can decide, as has been the practice in this country for a long time, to mitigate the fluctuations in oil prices, so that we can try to influence inflation, we can simply fill the budget or we can try to be competitive. So these are two cases where, in my opinion, the government has even more room for self-managing in the areas of public finances, revenues, taxes or expenditure on the basis of some legal powers. What we have proposed here with this amendment or a new article is much more restrictive, in my view, from the point of view of the space left by this article to the government. In my opinion, as I read this article, this article is very exact or very exact in determining what the government must or must do in these two respects. For the first time, it has to happen, this is actually a self-repairing mechanism. If we do not amend this law, there is no other way to avoid this increase in value added tax if the conditions laid down in this Article arise. At the same time, I think it is sufficiently clear, sufficiently precise, how much VAT is increased. This is said to be determined when, under what conditions. This is – let me read point 2 or point 1. If the government budget deficit for 2013 – let me stress this – is due next year, or for 2014 it will exceed 3% of the gross domestic product. What is a gross domestic product is clear, its scope is determined by the Statistical Office; what is 3% is probably also clear. So the condition, I think, is perfectly precise when comes, under which conditions this automatic value added tax increase takes place, which is the role of the automatic compensatory mechanism of public finances. By how much it increases and is also specified; it is increased by enough to provide additional revenue for the budget to cover this surplus deficit. The extent to which the deficit is pledged shall be determined by the final accounts of the budget for the previous year. The amount of the capital increase in the value added tax is a simple accounting operation that can be calculated, in my view, with a sufficient degree of precision, to be determined by a large amount of efficiency, to the extent that this value added tax rate has to be increased in order to achieve approximately such a financial impact as the deficit has been exceeded. All the Government will then do is calculate this and determine how much this VAT rate needs to be increased. However, in order to increase and what this calculation must be, but it is perfectly determined by this Article, it is said that the National Assembly, as the legislator of this government, instructs it to do so and, of course, also the power to calculate it. To make matters even less controversial, this is a temporary measure in force in the year that this condition is met. However, this is not a permanent change in the value added tax rate. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2012-05-10-SDZ6-Izredna-13.ana.u234	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. The question concerned the compatibility of the regulation with the Constitution in this part, where the amendment provided for the power of the Government to determine the tax rate. The legislative and legal service has not specifically highlighted the power of the Government to determine the rate of tax, given that such legal powers are not new to the Government in the field of taxation. As Mr Minister has already said, the Law on excise duties, for example, already authorizes the Government to reduce or increase excise duty on energy and electricity or, for example, excise duty on alcohol and alcoholic beverages. For example, the Government’s comparable power to modify the tax burden was already exercised in the taxation of the Transport Tax Act, paragraph 77 of which provided that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia could reduce or increase the rate of turnover of certain oil derivatives to 50% in order to prevent and remedy disturbances on the market or economy. With regard to compliance with this constitutional provision, which states that the State by law prescribes taxes, we consider that this amendment, the so-called legal regime, already provides for a possible range. However, the Legislative and Legal Service – my colleague has already pointed out on the Committee that such a solution, which would rather empower the Government, is unclear in the part which the factors are that determine this increase within a possible range. In our view, this would fully remove any doubts about the constitutionality of any provision, although I must say that the constitutional provision is very general and it is difficult here to assess whether this obligation to provide for a tax by law relates to all elements of the tax and is, of course, not possible — given the prior determination of these criteria, which I have mentioned — this power to give to the Government. However, I must also point out that such an authorised provision, which already exists in the legal order, has not yet been subject to an assessment from the point of view of the establishment. There are no solid elements here for us to be able to say decisively that this is constitutional or unconstitutional. However, as it is said, these factors, which would determine which factors are the ones that determine the increase in the range, would indeed eliminate this ambiguity and the possible unconstitutionality. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2012-05-10-SDZ6-Izredna-13.ana.u236	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Nice greetings to all ministers, government teams, Members and Members of Parliament! Today we are witnessing quite a few things to the obvious how this government and this coalition are incompetent, how we know nothing, and we have heard many times that we are not present in this hall. A few minutes ago also Members of Parliament and Members of Positive Slovenia were not present, and perhaps this is also the reason why you did not take the views given to you on your tables in your discussions. In fact, I'm going to take my colleague's words from the Finance Committee: "We're here to work, not to sleep." Although the president has already said once that we should not use this. However, with the evidence that they were flying to the government and the proposed measures, you were actually deceiving the public, because the amendments you received on your bench put many of your things in a different dimension, not to say a lie. Let me continue my debate. Many times in your debates were mentioned, by the Positive Slovenia and the opposition, that the European Union does not require such drastic measures in one year. Of course not, and it has already been said many times by the Government and the Minister of Finance as well as the coalition members that Slovenia committed itself in 2009 to reducing the country's deficit below 3% of the social gross product by 2013. If Mr Pahor's previous government had done so, of course, today we would not have had the balance of public finances on our hands, and Slovenia would be in a completely different situation than it is. And why is it important to balance public consumption? It is important because we are currently in charge of something that we can currently use, but we have not created it. In the last three years, the public sector has thus spent 20% more than it has created. This means that the government deficit has increased to 6.4% of the social gross product, which means that every year we spent 2 billion more than we created. For this reason, it is essential that the consumption of the public sector is framed into a framework that is realistic. But the real thing is what we create, and we can use it. We have heard many times in the debates that we will spend almost 700 million euros per year this year, instead of spending them on development, education and to increase the welfare of citizens, we will spend them on interest. Only for interest this year will we pay as much as has been said to be able to pay all pensioners 2 additional pensions, or we will pay scholarships to all students and students for this money for two years. We have also heard many times today that we are putting an end to the social state, public health, and I do not know what else. These are prophetic-demago statements about the dismantling and abolition of the social state, which are expected but far from the truth. They are rhetoric used by the opposition and thus part of the professional public in order to block the government in its efforts to get the country out of the crisis. What we really want to end is the abolition of a wasteful, inefficient and criminal state, and by no means a social state. With these measures, the Government does not actually take anything from anyone, but there is no money in society to continue to pay for some superstandard services. Perhaps in the future, when we have better financial results, we can, of course, go back to the situation four or three years ago. It must be recognized that this law does not bring either great winners or great losers. It's an evolution in the field of expenditure. It is not just that anyone wants to defeat someone, but that consumption adjusts to the real state. If we do not act, this means that soon there will be no social state for anyone else. Slovenia, as has been highlighted several times, is a social state and cannot be a social institution. Let me conclude by saying that I can agree with those who say that if the government and the coalition give in and withdraw the proposed law, then Slovenia has no help. Thank you.
